This is a two-mode memory buffer, because a regenerative dendritic calcium plateau lets a brief input latch into seconds of sustained output.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Resting Bridge - the Idle Span - the quiescent state near minus 70 to minus 75 millivolts between events.

◦ Plateau Sustain - the Long Echo - a latched calcium plateau of 20 to 30 millivolts that keeps the cell firing for up to ten seconds after the input stops.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Resting Bridge: standard integrator behaviour, membrane time constant about 25 milliseconds, no sustained output.

◦ Plateau Sustain: voltage-gated calcium channels carry a slow interspike ramp, holding a plateau lasting 300 to 3000 milliseconds and driving parvalbumin basket cell somata, mossy cells and hilar interneurons into barrages up to ten seconds long.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Brief perforant-path burst above about minus 60 millivolts - Resting Bridge to Plateau Sustain - milliseconds to latch.

◦ Dendritic inhibition - Plateau Sustain back to Resting Bridge - ends the plateau early.

◦ Plateau decay - self-timed return - 300 to 3000 milliseconds intrinsic.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Runaway plateaus - boundary signature is plateaus stretching past their normal duration - watchdog is dendritic inhibition and astrocytic potassium clearance; runaway plateaus could seed seizures, and losing the cell removes short-term binding and weakens feed-forward inhibition.


ENTRY 39. MOSSY CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 39)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-poor sparse relay of a busy input, because an adaptive climbing threshold turns a torrent of excitation into a thin output with no inhibition required.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Drowned Integrator - the Flooded Listener - the working state in which the cell receives hundreds of excitatory events per second yet is held sparse by a rising threshold.

◦ Detonation Relay - the One-For-One Shout - occasional near-detonator transmission where one strong presynaptic spike drives one postsynaptic spike.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Drowned Integrator: 30 to 50 giant mossy-fibre synapses plus spontaneous events at 100 to 500 hertz, but each spike raises threshold by 4 to 8 millivolts, recovering over 200 to 400 milliseconds, so output stays sparse.

◦ Detonation Relay: giant boutons make transmission strong enough that a single input can carry the cell across threshold, broadcasting glutamate 2 to 6 millimetres along the long axis.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Excitatory barrage overcoming the current threshold - Drowned Integrator to a spike - milliseconds.

◦ Threshold step after each spike - self-limiting return to Drowned Integrator - 200 to 400 milliseconds recovery.

◦ Septal and supramammillary modulation - shifts overall excitability - thrown from above by brain-state pathways.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Excitotoxic death - boundary signature is calcium overload under sustained barrage - watchdog is the adaptive threshold and astrocytic glutamate clearance; these cells die first in temporal lobe epilepsy and after head injury, removing long-range feedback inhibition.


ENTRY 40. CA3 PYRAMIDAL CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 40)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-rich recall engine, because intrinsic complex-spike bursting, regular firing and ripple participation are all genuine operating regimes of an autoassociative network.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Regular Single Firing - the Steady Report - single spikes from thorny cells during ordinary encoding.

◦ Complex-Spike Burst - the Recall Detonation - two to six spikes driven by calcium currents and stopped by calcium-activated potassium channels, prominent in athorny bursters.

◦ Ripple Volley - the Replay Beat - fast synchronised firing during sharp-wave ripples that replays stored patterns.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Regular Single Firing: resting near minus 68 millivolts, threshold near minus 50 millivolts, single spikes summing thousands of weak recurrent inputs.

◦ Complex-Spike Burst: intra-burst interval 5 to 15 milliseconds with an after-hyperpolarisation of 100 to 500 milliseconds; a mossy-fibre input can unblock nearby recurrent N-methyl-D-aspartate (NMDA) receptors so a subthreshold pattern becomes supralinear, completing a whole from a fragment.

◦ Ripple Volley: gap-junction coupling and dense packing let the population generate very fast ripples during rest and sleep.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Calcium recruitment by the primary sodium spike - Regular Single Firing to Complex-Spike Burst - milliseconds.

◦ Cholinergic tone from the septum - suppresses recurrent transmission during encoding and releases it during recall - thrown from above by brain state.

◦ Sharp-wave onset in rest and sleep - population enters Ripple Volley - set by the sleep-wake switch from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Seizure - boundary signature is excess recurrent gain - watchdog is fast perisomatic inhibition; CA3 is the most epileptogenic tissue in the brain, while too little gain gives failure of recall from partial cues.


ENTRY 41. CA2 PYRAMIDAL CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 41)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a deliberately mode-poor, hard-to-move relay, because a perineuronal net and a plasticity clamp keep it firing regularly rather than bursting, its register worth stating proudly as one main regime plus a neuromodulator-gated learning state.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Regular Tonic Firing - the Steady Social Ledger - even, non-bursting output resting near minus 70 millivolts, most active during immobility and slow-wave sleep.

◦ Peptide-Gated Plastic Window - the Permitted Change - a state in which vasopressin or oxytocin lifts the clamp so the Schaffer input may strengthen.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Regular Tonic Firing: low input resistance and threshold near minus 48 millivolts make the cell harder to excite than its neighbours, so it reports steadily rather than in packets, with its strong entorhinal tuft input as the main driver.

◦ Peptide-Gated Plastic Window: regulator of G protein signalling 14 and the perineuronal net clamp spine calcium to about 0.25, and vasopressin raises the effective gain about fourfold so accumulated calcium can cross the potentiation gate.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Vasopressin or oxytocin arrival, 0 to 100 nanomolar, partly by volume transmission - Regular Tonic Firing to Peptide-Gated Plastic Window - seconds to minutes, thrown from above by hypothalamic and septal peptide systems.

◦ Peptide washout - back to Regular Tonic Firing - seconds to minutes.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Loss of social recognition - boundary signature is net degradation - watchdog is the perineuronal net and calcium buffering that also make this the one hippocampal island resistant to seizure and stroke; net loss in schizophrenia, autism and Alzheimer's disease removes people while sparing places.


ENTRY 42. CA1 PYRAMIDAL CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 42)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is one of the great mode-rich integrators, because regular firing, complex bursting, a theta resonance and a dendritic plateau are all distinct working regimes of the hippocampus's main output cell.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Regular Single Firing - the Faithful Output - single spikes reporting the comparison of CA3 prediction against cortical report.

◦ Complex-Spike Burst - the Emphatic Packet - short high-frequency bursts that a downstream cell reads as a stronger symbol.

◦ Theta Resonance - the Rhythm Lock - a preferential response at 4 to 10 hertz set by distance-dependent dendritic channels.

◦ Dendritic Plateau - the One-Pass Place Field - a seconds-long calcium plateau that builds a place field in a single lap.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Regular Single Firing: resting near minus 68 millivolts, input resistance 50 to 100 megaohms, threshold near minus 50 millivolts.

◦ Complex-Spike Burst: bursts summed from Schaffer and tuft inputs, sharpened during ripples by patchy axonal myelin.

◦ Theta Resonance: channels growing denser with distance give a 4 to 10 hertz resonance that phases firing to the theta cycle.

◦ Dendritic Plateau: a plateau of 100 to 500 milliseconds opens an eligibility trace of 1 to 3 seconds, so behavioural-timescale plasticity strengthens every synapse active near it.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Coincident tuft and Schaffer drive - Regular Single Firing to Dendritic Plateau - hundreds of milliseconds.

◦ Somatic calcium recruitment - Regular Single Firing to Complex-Spike Burst - milliseconds.

◦ Neuromodulator setting, acetylcholine, noradrenaline, serotonin, dopamine - selects encoding versus recall - thrown from above by arousal and reward systems.

◦ Sharp-wave onset in rest - biases bursts into ripple timing, 5 to 7 millisecond period - set from above by sleep-wake state.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Ischaemic death - boundary signature is calcium overload with every burst - watchdog is glial D-serine and lactate supply; this is the first cell to die after cardiac arrest, the Sommer sector, and is lost early in Alzheimer's disease where ripples become disordered.


ENTRY 43. SUBICULAR PYRAMIDAL CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 43)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a two-mode output gate whose burst-versus-single-spike choice is itself a code, since bursting and regular-firing cells sit side by side and carry different amounts of spatial information.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Regular Spiking - the Plain Bulletin - single-spike output from the roughly regular-firing minority.

◦ Low-Threshold Burst - the Memory Packet - a tight packet of two to five spikes on a calcium-driven after-depolarisation that a target cannot ignore.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Regular Spiking: rest near minus 65 millivolts, threshold near minus 50 millivolts, input resistance 30 to 60 megaohms, output as evenly spaced spikes carrying the weaker spatial signal.

◦ Low-Threshold Burst: low-threshold Cav3 calcium channels fire a burst only after the cell has been held quiet long enough to recover, intra-burst interval 5 to 15 milliseconds, terminated by calcium-activated potassium channels over 200 to 300 milliseconds; about 20 percent of cells are dominant bursters carrying the stronger spatial signal.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Input arriving after a quiet interval that de-inactivates Cav3 - Regular Spiking to Low-Threshold Burst - Cav3 recovery 100 to 200 milliseconds.

◦ Back-propagating spike meeting coincident CA1 and cortical input - triggers dendritic calcium spikes - milliseconds.

◦ Neuromodulation by acetylcholine, noradrenaline, serotonin and, ventrally, dopamine - sets excitability and the stress-axis output - thrown from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Interictal spiking - boundary signature is loss of the chloride transporter KCC2 so gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) depolarises - watchdog is chloride handling; the subiculum then becomes the interictal spike generator of temporal lobe epilepsy, and age-related attrition narrows the hippocampus's only output channel.


ENTRY 44. ENTORHINAL STELLATE CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 44)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a great mode-rich resonator, because a standing Ih current gives it subthreshold theta oscillation, membrane resonance and cholinergic persistent firing, the cellular ingredients of the grid map.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta Subthreshold Oscillation - the Idle Hum - a self-generated 4 to 12 hertz membrane oscillation just below threshold.

◦ Resonant Spiking - the Tuned Beat - spikes phased to inputs near the resonance frequency, which shifts along the dorsal-to-ventral axis with grid spacing.

◦ Persistent Firing - the Self-Held Trace - cholinergically switched self-sustained firing for tens of seconds with no synaptic help.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Theta Subthreshold Oscillation: HCN1 channels carrying Ih make the depolarised membrane unstable and oscillate in the theta band, membrane time constant 8 to 15 milliseconds.

◦ Resonant Spiking: the membrane acts as a band-pass filter peaking at 7 to 10 hertz dorsally and 3 to 5 hertz ventrally, so identical input delivered at theta produces a far larger swing than at 1 hertz.

◦ Persistent Firing: a calcium-activated non-specific cation current under muscarinic control holds firing for tens of seconds.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Depolarisation from rest toward threshold - Theta Subthreshold Oscillation to Resonant Spiking - within a theta cycle of 125 to 250 milliseconds.

◦ Acetylcholine from the medial septum - any mode to Persistent Firing, and shifts resonance frequency - thrown from above by the cholinergic system.

◦ Hyperpolarisation revealing sag - primes rebound spikes after a delay set by resonance frequency - HCN activation 20 to 200 milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Tangle-driven death - boundary signature is neurofibrillary tangle accumulation - watchdog is the sodium-potassium pump undoing the standing Ih leak; layer two of the entorhinal cortex is the first cortical region to tangle, having already lost about 60 percent of its neurons in very mild Alzheimer's disease, the clinical result being getting lost in familiar places.


ENTRY 45. ENTORHINAL FAN CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 45)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a patient integrator with a memory mode, an excitable low-sag cell that binds what to where and can hold an item in mind through cholinergic persistent firing.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Silent Rest - the Empty Slate - the quiescent state between binding events.

◦ Regular Accommodating Firing - the Steady Binder - regular spike trains that slow during maintained input and hand object-place-context bundles to the dentate gyrus.

◦ Persistent Firing - the Held Item - self-sustaining firing lasting more than thirty seconds under acetylcholine.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Silent Rest: rest near minus 68.5 millivolts, input resistance about 130 megaohms, membrane time constant about 24 milliseconds, sag ratio near 0.85, marking an integrator rather than a resonator.

◦ Regular Accommodating Firing: high resistance and slow time constant make the cell excitable and patient, firing sparse competitive trains, 5 to 15 percent of the local population active at once.

◦ Persistent Firing: a calcium-activated non-specific cation current switched on by acetylcholine holds an input in a self-sustaining loop for 30 seconds or more.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Perirhinal and piriform drive crossing threshold - Silent Rest to Regular Accommodating Firing - membrane time constant about 24 milliseconds.

◦ Acetylcholine level rising toward 1 - Regular Accommodating Firing to Persistent Firing - thrown from above by the cholinergic system, which is why anticholinergics blur episodic memory.

◦ Shared inhibitory pool, 0 to 8 nanosiemens - enforces competition and returns most cells to Silent Rest - 5 to 10 milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Early tangle loss - boundary signature is the earliest cortical tau pathology - watchdog is none intrinsic; fan cells are among the first human neurons to tangle and die, and loss of cholinergic input removes persistent firing and with it the ability to hold an object across a delay.


ENTRY 46. PARVALBUMIN BASKET CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 46)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a great mode-rich rhythm generator that is nonetheless nearly single-purpose, because its fast-spiking output takes on distinct rhythmic identities as gamma and ripple clocks under brain-state control.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Quiescent Ready - the Poised Metronome - the resting state between recruited rhythms, coincidence-detecting but silent.

◦ Gamma Clocking - the Background Tick - sustained fast spiking that chops excitation into 30 to 80 hertz windows.

◦ Ripple Locking - the Replay Clock - very fast, tightly phase-locked firing during sharp-wave ripples.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Quiescent Ready: rest near minus 65 millivolts, low input resistance 60 to 120 megaohms and short time constant 8 to 12 milliseconds, so the cell fires only when many inputs arrive within a few milliseconds.

◦ Gamma Clocking: Kv3 and Nav1.1 channels support 0.3 millisecond spikes at 100 to 250 per second with almost no adaptation, delivering fast alpha1 gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) type A inhibition decaying in 2 to 5 milliseconds onto somata across the local population near-simultaneously.

◦ Ripple Locking: connexin 36 gap junctions and ephaptic coupling synchronise the network faster than chemical synapses allow.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Recurrent pyramidal drive closing the excitation-inhibition loop - Quiescent Ready to Gamma Clocking - within one gamma cycle of 12 to 30 milliseconds.

◦ Sharp-wave input from CA3 in rest - Gamma Clocking to Ripple Locking - set from above by the sleep-wake and arousal state.

◦ Cholinergic excitation and opioid inhibition - raise or lower recruitment - thrown from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Runaway excitation - boundary signature is loss of the millisecond clock - watchdog is the perineuronal net buffering ions and oxidative stress; in schizophrenia these cells lose parvalbumin and nets and gamma falls, in Alzheimer's disease reduced Nav1.1 slows them, and in epilepsy their loss removes the only inhibition fast enough to stop a seizure spreading.


ENTRY 47. CHOLECYSTOKININ BASKET CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 47)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mood-tracking two-to-three mode cell whose defining regime is being switched off by the very cell it inhibits, the deliberate opposite of the parvalbumin metronome.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Slow Background Inhibition - the Mood Dial - regular accommodating firing that sets a slowly varying perisomatic tone tracking emotional state.

◦ Asynchronous Tail - the Lingering Leak - transmitter that keeps leaking out after the spike in proportion to residual calcium, blurring timing on purpose.

◦ Endocannabinoid Suppression - the Ordered Silence - a seconds-long shut-down imposed retrogradely by the target pyramidal cell.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Slow Background Inhibition: broad 0.6 to 1.0 millisecond spikes, high input resistance 150 to 300 megaohms, tens rather than hundreds of spikes per second, preferring the ascending theta phase opposite to parvalbumin cells.

◦ Asynchronous Tail: N-type calcium channels sit far from the release machinery, giving a baseline release probability of 0.4 to 0.5 and an asynchronous tail of 20 to 60 milliseconds, so the terminal is not a clean digital device.

◦ Endocannabinoid Suppression: a depolarised target makes 2-arachidonoylglycerol that binds CB1 receptors, the densest in the brain, closing N-type channels and silencing release for 5 to 20 seconds.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Serotonin on 5-HT3A receptors or acetylcholine on muscarinic receptors - raises firing within milliseconds - thrown from above by mood and arousal systems.

◦ Postsynaptic depolarisation - Slow Background Inhibition to Endocannabinoid Suppression - onset within a second, recovery 5 to 20 seconds.

◦ Residual calcium after a spike - sustains the Asynchronous Tail - 20 to 60 milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Prolonged silencing - boundary signature is CB1 receptors flooded for hours - watchdog is normal endocannabinoid clearance; cannabis silences these terminals far past the natural seconds, producing the short-term memory loss users report, and dysfunction here is implicated in anxiety disorders.


ENTRY 48. AXO-AXONIC CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 48)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a state-switched veto cell, fast-spiking like the basket cell but defined by a clean alternation between theta-locked firing and total ripple silence.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta-Locked Veto - the Gate Closed - fast spiking on the descending phase just after the theta peak, holding the veto over pyramidal initial segments.

◦ Ripple Silence - the Gate Lifted - complete silence during sharp-wave ripples, exactly when pyramidal cells fire hardest.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Theta-Locked Veto: brief spikes, sustained rates above 100 per second, input resistance 60 to 150 megaohms, delivering gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) only to axon initial segments with a depolarised chloride reversal near minus 55 millivolts that clamps and shunts without exciting.

◦ Ripple Silence: long-range inhibition switches the cell off so the initial-segment brake is withdrawn, permitting memory replay.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Theta phase on the descending slope - drives Theta-Locked Veto - theta cycle 125 to 250 milliseconds.

◦ Sharp-wave ripple onset - Theta-Locked Veto to Ripple Silence - ripple episode 50 to 100 milliseconds, thrown from above by long-range inhibition and brain state.

◦ Target initial-segment remodelling - slowly resets veto strength - hours to days.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Brake-to-accelerator flip - boundary signature is chloride rising further at the initial segment - watchdog is the transporters KCC2 and NKCC1 setting the local gradient; in chronic temporal lobe epilepsy these cells are lost or disconnected, removing the last-line veto, and if chloride rises the synapse can push the segment above threshold instead of below.


ENTRY 49. BISTRATIFIED CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 49)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a near-fast-spiking dendritic restrainer with a strong ripple mode, gated to the band where CA3 traffic lands.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta-Trough Restraint - the Steady Curb - firing near the theta trough that holds down proximal-dendrite excitation as CA3 input arrives.

◦ Ripple Barrage - the Fierce Clamp - firing near 100 spikes per second during ripples, one to two milliseconds after the pyramidal cells.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Theta-Trough Restraint: input resistance 100 to 200 megaohms, half-millisecond spikes, feedforward and feedback inhibition delivered onto shafts and spines in oriens and radiatum across about 1600 target cells, stopping dead at stratum lacunosum-moleculare.

◦ Ripple Barrage: fast bursts add peptide release, somatostatin and neuropeptide Y above 40 hertz, and suppress dendritic calcium spikes, vetoing lasting change at particular branches.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ CA3 Schaffer drive plus local pyramidal feedback crossing threshold - Theta-Trough Restraint to firing - inhibitory decay 4 to 8 milliseconds.

◦ Sharp-wave ripple onset - Theta-Trough Restraint to Ripple Barrage - ripple cycle 5 to 8 milliseconds, set from above by brain state.

◦ Theta-locked septal gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) - paces the cell to the trough - thrown from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Seizure spread - boundary signature is loss of the dendritic clamp on ripples - watchdog is the perineuronal net buffering ions during furious firing; its loss frees CA3 drive and feeds temporal lobe epilepsy, and parvalbumin failure is suspected in schizophrenia and degraded Alzheimer ripples.


ENTRY 50. ORIENS-LACUNOSUM MOLECULARE (O-LM) CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 50)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-rich theta resonator, because an h-current tunes it to theta, facilitating feedback recruits it only on a burst, and glutamate-plus-acetylcholine can latch it into a plateau.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta-Paced Idle - the Slow Sway - rhythmic subthreshold behaviour set by the h-current, tuned near theta and deaf to other rates.

◦ Recruited Tuft Filtering - the Tightening Filter - firing that damps the distal pyramidal tuft, growing with each theta-frequency spike.

◦ Plateau Sustain - the Held Shoulder - a plateau potential of hundreds of milliseconds triggered by glutamate together with acetylcholine.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Theta-Paced Idle: input resistance 200 to 400 megaohms, membrane time constant 20 to 40 milliseconds, a sag ratio of 1.2 to 1.5 marking the h-current, so the membrane resonates rather than adds.

◦ Recruited Tuft Filtering: local CA1 collaterals facilitate through Elfn1 and metabotropic glutamate receptor 7, holding release probability low so only a burst recruits the cell, which then inhibits about 1457 distal tufts and blocks tuft calcium spikes.

◦ Plateau Sustain: coincident glutamate and acetylcholine trigger a plateau turning a brief input into a sustained output.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Facilitating pyramidal burst crossing threshold - Theta-Paced Idle to Recruited Tuft Filtering - facilitation 150 to 300 milliseconds.

◦ Glutamate plus acetylcholine - firing to Plateau Sustain - thrown partly from above by the cholinergic system.

◦ Theta-locked septal gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) - keeps the cell paced to theta - thrown from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Loss of purposeful gating of the world - boundary signature is every sensory detail pushing into recall - watchdog is facilitation-gated recruitment; somatostatin interneurons are lost early in Alzheimer's disease, and fewer of them tracks with faster decline and degraded theta.


ENTRY 51. IVY CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 51)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-poor slow mist-maker, the commonest hippocampal interneuron, whose late-spiking output sets a diffuse background hush and can be switched off wholesale by opioids.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Opioid-Silenced Rest - the Muted Fog - a quiescent state imposed when mu-opioid receptors hyperpolarise the cell.

◦ Late-Spiking Hush - the Slow Mist - late-spiking output at one or two hertz that releases gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) as a slow cloud over a neighbourhood.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Opioid-Silenced Rest: mu-opioid agonists hyperpolarise the cell 3 to 10 millivolts, stripping out its diffuse inhibition.

◦ Late-Spiking Hush: a potassium brake delays the first spike by 50 to 300 milliseconds, input resistance 250 to 500 megaohms, then GABA spills over a 20 to 60 micrometre radius by volume transmission with neuropeptide Y and on-demand nitric oxide, decaying over 50 to 150 milliseconds.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ CA3 Schaffer and pyramidal feedback wearing off the brake - Opioid-Silenced Rest or quiescence to Late-Spiking Hush - first-spike delay 50 to 300 milliseconds.

◦ Mu-opioid occupancy - Late-Spiking Hush to Opioid-Silenced Rest - fast, thrown from above by opioid signalling.

◦ Gap-junction current from neurogliaform and other interneurons - nudges the coupled sheet together - continuous.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Neurovascular uncoupling - boundary signature is failed nitric oxide signalling - watchdog is astrocytic GABA transporters setting cloud spread; opioids strip out diffuse inhibition and let excitability drift up, and failed nitric oxide uncouples activity from blood flow in dementia.


ENTRY 52. HIPPOCAMPAL NEUROGLIAFORM CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 52)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a two-mode onset broadcaster, remarkable as the one interneuron that evokes a slow metabotropic response from a single spike.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Braked Silence - the Held Cloud - the late-spiking resting state as a slow potassium brake holds the cell below threshold.

◦ Single-Spike Blanket - the One-Shot Fog - a spike that fogs a sphere of tissue with a biphasic, seconds-reaching inhibition.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Braked Silence: input resistance 200 to 400 megaohms, first-spike delay 50 to 200 milliseconds, strong accommodation so the cell reports onset rather than duration.

◦ Single-Spike Blanket: one spike releases a prolonged low-concentration gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) transient reaching every cell within 50 to 150 micrometres, a fast component rising in 2.8 and decaying in 51 milliseconds plus a metabotropic component about one fifth as large rising in 45 and decaying in 180 milliseconds.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Entorhinal layer three or CA3 excitation wearing off the brake - Braked Silence to Single-Spike Blanket - first-spike delay 50 to 200 milliseconds.

◦ Astrocytic GABA transporters clearing the cloud - return to Braked Silence - clearance 50 to 200 milliseconds.

◦ Gap-junction coupling in about 85 percent of pairs - locks the population as one element - continuous.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Release of the direct sensory path - boundary signature is loss of slow inhibition at the entorhinal input zone - watchdog is astrocytic clearance and gap-junction pooling; losing this slow blanket is implicated in temporal lobe epilepsy, and opioids suppress these cells.


ENTRY 53. HILAR PERFORANT PATH-ASSOCIATED (HIPP) CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 53)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a feedback burst detector, a slow regular cell recruited only by converging granule-cell bursts, whose peptide output is its distinctive second mode.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Exploratory Regular Firing - the Rising Watch - slow regular spiking that increases when the animal explores.

◦ Burst-Gated Peptide Release - the Anticonvulsant Puff - somatostatin and neuropeptide Y released only on a real burst, suppressing the perforant-path terminals.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Exploratory Regular Firing: input resistance 200 to 400 megaohms, membrane time constant 25 to 40 milliseconds, broad spikes with a large after-hyperpolarisation, rest near minus 62 millivolts.

◦ Burst-Gated Peptide Release: facilitating mossy-fibre collaterals with a facilitation ceiling near five recruit the cell only when converging bursts arrive, roughly four spikes in 50 milliseconds, whereupon somatostatin and neuropeptide Y diffuse onto perforant terminals for seconds by volume transmission.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Converging granule-cell burst overcoming the facilitating threshold - Exploratory Regular Firing to Burst-Gated Peptide Release - facilitation 200 to 300 milliseconds.

◦ Septal acetylcholine - raises firing during exploration - thrown from above.

◦ Scattered, non-converging input - keeps the cell in Exploratory Regular Firing without peptide release - fast.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Excitotoxic death - boundary signature is calcium overload in poorly capillarised tissue that buffers calcium badly - watchdog is facilitation gating and neuropeptide Y's own anticonvulsant action; HIPP and mossy cells are the most vulnerable hilar populations in temporal lobe epilepsy, and their loss defines hippocampal sclerosis, removing pattern separation itself.


ENTRY 54. HILAR COMMISSURAL-ASSOCIATIONAL PATHWAY-RELATED (HICAP) CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 54)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is an accommodating, cannabinoid-gated feedback curb on the dentate's own recurrent drive, whose retrograde suppression mode mirrors the cortical cholecystokinin basket cell.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Accommodating Curb - the Loose Rein - regular accommodating firing whose intervals lengthen during maintained input, holding down inner-molecular-layer excitation.

◦ Asynchronous Tail - the Lingering Spill - gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) that keeps spilling past the spike from loosely coupled release machinery.

◦ Endocannabinoid Suppression - the Ordered Hush - a seconds-long silencing thrown by a depolarised granule cell.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Accommodating Curb: input resistance 150 to 350 megaohms, membrane time constant 15 to 30 milliseconds, broad spikes, baseline release probability 0.3 to 0.4 onto granule-cell proximal dendrites.

◦ Asynchronous Tail: terminal calcium sustains release for tens of milliseconds after the spike, emptying vesicle pools so terminals refill constantly.

◦ Endocannabinoid Suppression: a depolarised granule cell makes endocannabinoids that mute these terminals for 2 to 10 seconds, release probability falling toward zero.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Granule-cell collateral drive plus commissural input crossing threshold - rest to Accommodating Curb - adaptation 100 to 300 milliseconds.

◦ Postsynaptic granule-cell depolarisation - Accommodating Curb to Endocannabinoid Suppression - suppression 2 to 10 seconds.

◦ Terminal calcium after a spike - sustains the Asynchronous Tail - 50 to 100 milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Runaway feedback - boundary signature is loss of control over the granule-to-mossy-cell loop - watchdog is retrograde endocannabinoid gain control; cannabinoids suppress these terminals and impair encoding, and in epilepsy models mutual inhibition among these cells is strengthened.


ENTRY 55. MOLECULAR LAYER PERFORANT PATH-ASSOCIATED (MOPP) CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 55)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a feed-forward pre-emptive brake, a near-linear single-lamina timing device that fires before the granule cells and can also broadcast a slow tissue-wide tone.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Feed-Forward Pre-Empt - the Not-Yet - regular spiking that fires 3 to 6 milliseconds ahead of the granule cells to brake an incoming message while it is still arriving.

◦ Slow Volume Tone - the Lingering Hush - spillover gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) acting on GABA type B and delta-subunit GABA type A receptors, lasting hundreds of milliseconds.

◦ Late-Spiking Onset - the Neurogliaform Hesitation - a late-spiking regime in the neurogliaform-like subset that reports onset only.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Feed-Forward Pre-Empt: input resistance 150 to 400 megaohms, rest near minus 65 millivolts, broad 0.6 to 1.2 millisecond spikes, maximum 20 to 60 hertz, delivering fast GABA type A of 0.5 to 2 nanosiemens per spike onto granule-cell middle and outer dendrites with a feed-forward latency of 3 to 6 milliseconds.

◦ Slow Volume Tone: spillover gives 0.1 to 0.4 nanosiemens of slow extrasynaptic conductance per spike over 150 to 300 milliseconds, plus nitric oxide and neuropeptide Y as diffusible fields over 20 to 100 micrometres; release is markedly asynchronous.

◦ Late-Spiking Onset: the neurogliaform-like members hesitate before the first spike and report onset rather than duration.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Perforant-path glutamate, 0 to 20 nanosiemens - rest to Feed-Forward Pre-Empt - alpha-amino-3-hydroxy-5-methyl-4-isoxazolepropionic acid (AMPA) rise 0.5 milliseconds, ahead of the granule cells.

◦ Repeated firing accumulating spillover - Feed-Forward Pre-Empt to Slow Volume Tone - GABA type B 150 to 300 milliseconds.

◦ Septal GABAergic and cholinergic tone, and endocannabinoid suppression in the total-molecular-layer relatives - modulate output - thrown from above and by granule-cell retrograde signals.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Loss of the first refusal - boundary signature is the entorhinal barrage reaching granule dendrites unopposed, widening the firing window to tens of milliseconds - watchdog is the feed-forward latency itself; in temporal lobe epilepsy loss of dentate inhibition drives synchronous discharge, and in Alzheimer's disease amyloid degrades this exact terminal zone before the cell's own machinery fails.


ENTRY 56. TRILAMINAR CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 56)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a state-broadcasting disinhibitor whose whole register is set from above, since brain state alone dials its rate between a slow-wave-sleep burster and a near-silent dreamer.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Slow-Wave Burst - the Sleep Beat - high-rate bursting during dreamless sleep that intermittently releases parvalbumin basket cells from control.

◦ Waking Tonic - the Quiet-Watch Rate - steady firing during quiet wakefulness and movement.

◦ Rapid-Eye-Movement Hush - the Dreamer's Silence - near-silence when acetylcholine is high.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ Slow-Wave Burst: about 21.6 hertz mean rate in slow-wave sleep with intra-burst rates above 200 hertz and interspike intervals under 9 milliseconds, output landing chiefly on interneuron dendrites, 59 to 84 percent of targets, so inhibiting inhibitors disinhibits CA1, plus a fast myelinated bulletin to the subiculum at 1 to 2 metres per second.

◦ Waking Tonic: about 19 hertz in quiet wakefulness and 8.5 hertz during movement, firing on the ascending theta slope near 84 degrees, almost opposite the somatostatin projection cells (detailed in vitro resistance and time-constant numbers are scarce and should not be invented).

◦ Rapid-Eye-Movement Hush: about 1.8 hertz, the cell turned down by acetylcholine through the inhibitory M2 muscarinic receptor.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Onset of slow-wave sleep - Waking Tonic to Slow-Wave Burst, bursting about 2.5 times more frequent - thrown from above by sleep-wake state.

◦ Rising acetylcholine in movement and rapid-eye-movement sleep - any mode to Rapid-Eye-Movement Hush via M2 - thrown from above by the cholinergic system.

◦ Group-three metabotropic glutamate receptors mGluR8a and mGluR7a on septal GABAergic terminals - let ambient glutamate reduce inhibition onto the cell - presynaptic cross-talk.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Loss of state-dependent gating - boundary signature is failure to release parvalbumin basket cells at 200 hertz burst timing during sleep replay - watchdog is M2 cholinergic gain control; no distinct pathological regime is firmly established for this rare, incompletely studied cell, and that gap is stated rather than filled.
MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS ENTRIES

Neuron catalogue Chapters 57 to 86 (hippocampal formation, amygdala, piriform and claustrum, striatum, pallidum, subthalamic nucleus, substantia nigra, ventral tegmental area, nucleus accumbens, and thalamic relay and local cells).

Keyed for mechanical splice into the source volume.


ENTRY 57. BACK-PROJECTION CELLS (Upstream-Feedback Muting Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 57)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a two-mode, brain-state-slaved integrator whose modes are set by the hippocampal rhythm above it and whose input synapse, not its membrane, does the counting.

It is a burst detector, so it has no meaningful always-on regime of its own.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta-Tick - the Background Tick - a low steady discharge that tracks exploration and keeps the feedback synapse primed.

◦ Ripple-Report Burst - the Closing Bell - a high-rate volley during sharp wave ripples (the 150 to 200 hertz replay bursts) that tells area CA3 and the dentate gyrus that CA1 has produced an answer.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Background Tick the cell fires about 4 hertz, phase-preferring theta, with the presynaptic release probability of its CA1 pyramidal input sitting low near 0.03 so isolated spikes are ignored.

◦ In the Closing Bell the cell fires about 23 hertz, released once release probability has facilitated toward 0.6 across a pyramidal train, and only here does it dump somatostatin and neuropeptide Y as slow diffusible fields; rate figures are from identified rodent cells and are moderate confidence.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Sharp wave ripple onset in CA1 - Background Tick to Closing Bell - within a few tens of milliseconds; this is thrown from above, since the exploration-versus-replay state is a hippocampal and septal broadcast, not a local choice.

◦ Return to locomotion or rapid eye movement sleep theta - Closing Bell to Background Tick - one to a few theta cycles.

◦ Facilitation decay of the input synapse - silences the cell between bursts - about 200 milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Silent-loss regime: in temporal lobe epilepsy these somatostatin-positive cells are selectively lost, the boundary signature is falling dentate and CA3 inhibition, and the watchdog is that experimentally silencing hippocampal somatostatin interneurons alone triggers recurrent seizures, so their survival is itself the safety margin.


ENTRY 58. HIPPOCAMPO-SEPTAL PROJECTING INHIBITORY CELLS (Exploration-Curbing Long-Range Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 58)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a two-mode delay line that speaks back to the medial septum, and its population is split so cleanly by brain state that the split is effectively the register.

Its myelinated axon makes the loop delay a computational parameter rather than a nuisance.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta-On Locking - the Exploration Voice - phase-locked firing during active exploration that reports the hippocampus back to the septal pacemaker.

◦ Ripple-On Burst - the Replay Herald - a high-rate volley that begins before the sharp wave ripple and peaks before the ripple peak.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Exploration Voice, theta-on cells fire in phase with the theta cycle at modest rates, while the double-projecting subset fires only about 0.7 hertz during theta; confidence is low, from six identified cells.

◦ In the Replay Herald the double-projecting subset jumps to about 40 hertz during sharp wave ripples, and the theta-off subset, near silent in locomotion and rapid eye movement sleep, leads the ripple.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Sharp wave ripple initiation - Exploration Voice to Replay Herald - leads the ripple by a few to tens of milliseconds; thrown from above by the hippocampal state switch.

◦ Onset of theta locomotion or rapid eye movement sleep - Replay Herald to Exploration Voice, and near silence in the theta-off subset - within a theta cycle.

◦ Facilitating pyramidal input crossing burst threshold - drives either firing mode from rest - tens of milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ These are among the most vulnerable cells in epilepsy and their loss is not compensated; the warning condition is an open septo-hippocampal loop in which theta becomes an externally imposed metronome, and there is no cell-intrinsic watchdog beyond the septal feedback that normally regulates them.


ENTRY 59. RADIATUM-RETROHIPPOCAMPAL CELLS (Early-Arriving Calming Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 59)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-poor, theta-time channel: it is essentially a one-and-a-half mode cell that runs during theta and, unusually for a hippocampal long-range cell, does not join the replay chorus.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Theta-Time Veto - the Early Calmer - a steady descending-phase discharge during theta that delivers a hippocampal veto onto distant retrosplenial and subicular apical tufts.

◦ Ripple-Quiet - the Replay Holiday - a near-off state during sharp wave ripples, the opposite of trilaminar and hippocampo-septal behaviour.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Early Calmer the cell fires about 5.2 hertz, preferring the descending theta phase, blocking N-methyl-D-aspartate-dependent plateau potentials in its cortical targets; from two identified cells, low confidence.

◦ In the Replay Holiday firing falls to about 0.7 hertz and does not increase with ripples; same low confidence, expose as parameters.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Theta onset during exploration - rest to Early Calmer - within a theta cycle; a brain-state service imposed from above.

◦ Sharp wave ripple onset - Early Calmer to Replay Holiday - tens of milliseconds; also state-thrown.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ No characterised pathological firing regime exists for this cell, and its plasticity is largely unknown; the honest statement is that its main vulnerability is white matter disease of its long thin axon rather than a runaway firing mode.


ENTRY 60. AMYGDALA PRINCIPAL NEURONS (Threat-And-Reward Valuing Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 60)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-poor sparse integrator whose interest is plastic weight, not firing repertoire: it mostly runs one quiet accumulating mode with a strong adaptation brake.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Sparse Vigilance - the Quiet Appraiser - a sub-1 hertz strongly adapting discharge that samples thousands of cortical, thalamic and hippocampal inputs and assigns value.

◦ Associative Recall Burst - the Learned Alarm - brief higher-rate firing to a stimulus that training has paired with reward or threat.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Quiet Appraiser the cell rests at minus 65 to minus 75 millivolts, input resistance 100 to 250 megohms, fires under one spike per second, and adapts strongly, so only convergent input drives it.

◦ In the Learned Alarm a potentiated input pathway (strengthened by N-methyl-D-aspartate-dependent long-term potentiation) evokes a short spike run gated by dendritic N-methyl-D-aspartate spikes on thin branches.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Arrival of a conditioned cue on a potentiated pathway - Quiet Appraiser to Learned Alarm - milliseconds.

◦ Neuromodulatory tone from dopamine, noradrenaline, serotonin and acetylcholine - shifts gain and adaptation, setting how readily the alarm is thrown - seconds; arousal-linked and set from above.

◦ Strong spike-frequency adaptation - returns the cell to Quiet Appraiser - tens to hundreds of milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Runaway associative potentiation is the pathological direction, seen as pathological fear generalisation; the watchdog is local parvalbumin and somatostatin inhibition plus endocannabinoid retrograde muting of that inhibition, which keeps the excitatory population editable rather than latched.


ENTRY 61. AMYGDALA INTERCALATED CELLS (Safety-Learning Brake Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 61)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a two-state switch rather than a dimmer, built from mutual inhibition between clusters and set by a standing chloride background.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Tonic Restraint - the Held Brake - continuous fast firing that keeps central amygdala output cells inhibited and fear circuits in check.

◦ Neuromodulatory Silence - the Released Latch - a switched-off state imposed by dopamine or opioids that lifts the brake.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Held Brake the cell fires 40 to 80 hertz with slight adaptation and high input resistance of 200 to 500 megohms, its gain set by a constant background chloride current through delta-subunit gamma-aminobutyric acid type A receptors.

◦ In the Released Latch, dopamine D1 or mu-opioid receptor activation silences the cell, and mutual inhibition between medial clusters snaps the whole group toward the same state.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Dopamine or enkephalin arriving by volume transmission - Held Brake to Released Latch - hundreds of milliseconds to seconds; effectively thrown from above by neuromodulatory systems and stress.

◦ Extinction-strengthened basolateral input - drives the Held Brake harder - milliseconds, with the learned change accruing over training.

◦ Clearance of the neuromodulator and cluster mutual inhibition - Released Latch back to Held Brake - seconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Failure of extinction is the clinical fault: the brake never strengthens, so fear cannot be shed; the background chloride conductance is the built-in volume knob and its loss is the boundary signature.


ENTRY 62. CENTRAL AMYGDALA NEURONS (Defence-Response Selecting Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 62)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-rich competitive selector, with three intrinsic firing types that, through mutual inhibition, turn a graded threat estimate into a discrete defensive choice.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Late-Firing Delay - the Held Breath - a delayed first spike that withholds output while evidence builds.

◦ Regular Spiking - the Steady Report - even firing that carries the ongoing defensive command.

◦ Low-Threshold Burst - the Rebound Trigger - a burst that fires on release from inhibition and commits to a response.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Held Breath a transient potassium current delays the first spike by 100 to 300 milliseconds, near 200 to 500 megohms input resistance.

◦ In the Steady Report the cell fires evenly and, at high rates, releases peptides (somatostatin, corticotropin releasing factor, enkephalin, dynorphin, neurotensin) so the same cell says different things when calm and when alarmed.

◦ In the Rebound Trigger a low-threshold calcium spike follows a period of inhibition and drives a short burst.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Rising basolateral and parabrachial drive - Held Breath to Steady Report - within the 100 to 300 millisecond delay window.

◦ Release from intercalated or intracluster inhibition - to Rebound Trigger - milliseconds; mutual inhibition forces a winner between freezing and fleeing.

◦ High firing sustained by stress - engages peptide co-release - seconds to weeks; stress-axis driven from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Parabrachial-to-lateral long-term potentiation in persistent pain models is a used-becomes-pathological direction; the watchdog is astrocytic calcium gating of excitation onto output cells, a rare case of glia inside the selection.


ENTRY 63. PIRIFORM CORTEX SEMILUNAR CELLS AND SUPERFICIAL PYRAMIDAL CELLS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 63)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

These are two integrator cell classes running a recurrent auto-associative network, and their shared mode axis is set by acetylcholine, which switches the cortex between learning a smell and recognising one.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Recognition Mode - the Known Scent - recurrent associational fibres active, completing a learned odour from a fragment.

◦ Acquisition Mode - the New Scent - cholinergic suppression of associational fibres so incoming bulb input is learned rather than pattern-completed.

◦ Sparse Encoding - the Thin Signature - the baseline sparse, adapting representation both classes hold to keep the recurrent network from igniting.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Known Scent, strong associational long-term potentiation lets far synapses count as much as near ones through dendritic amplification, and global inhibition holds the active-cell count steady.

◦ In the New Scent, acetylcholine from the diagonal band suppresses associational transmission while sparing lateral olfactory tract input; semilunar cells, with higher input resistance and weaker afterhyperpolarisation, sum longer and adapt less than superficial pyramidal cells.

◦ In the Thin Signature both classes fire sparsely and adapt, keeping the idling recurrent network's energy bill down.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Cholinergic tone from the diagonal band rising - Known Scent to New Scent - hundreds of milliseconds to seconds; an arousal and attention service set from above.

◦ Falling acetylcholine - New Scent back to Known Scent - seconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ The recurrent excitation makes piriform cortex famously epileptogenic (kindling-prone); the boundary signature is runaway pattern completion, and the watchdog is fast global feedback inhibition that clamps the active count.


ENTRY 64. CLAUSTRUM PROJECTION NEURONS (Engagement-Gating Sheet Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 64)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-poor broadcast relay whose interest is wiring, not firing repertoire: an ordinary regular-spiking cell that delivers one spike to several cortical lobes at once.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Regular Broadcast - the Chairman's Gavel - moderate regular firing that synchronously engages inhibitory cells across widely separated cortical areas.

◦ Neuromodulated Gain Shift - the Altered Chair - the same broadcast rescaled by serotonin 2A and kappa-opioid tone, given the cell's exceptional density of those receptors.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Chairman's Gavel the cell fires regularly with moderate adaptation near 100 to 300 megohms input resistance, its collaterals timed by capsular myelin so distant cortical areas are reached near-simultaneously.

◦ In the Altered Chair, serotonin 2A receptor activation (the target of classic psychedelics) or kappa-opioid tone reshapes excitability and thereby the reach of the broadcast; quantitative firing changes are not well characterised, low confidence.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Frontal cortical drive from anterior cingulate and prefrontal cortex - rest to Chairman's Gavel - milliseconds.

◦ Serotonergic or opioid neuromodulation - shifts to the Altered Chair - seconds; set from above by arousal and pharmacology.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ No canonical plasticity rule or pathological firing mode is established for this cell; honestly, its register is thin, and its core parvalbumin gatekeepers, locked by perineuronal nets, are the main stabiliser.


ENTRY 65. HARM-CONVERGING GATEKEEPER NEURON (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 65)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a proposed and unconfirmed (predicted) construct: a hysteretic coincidence gate whose two states are separated by a latch, so it is a two-mode switch with memory.

Every number here is predicted and should be marked low confidence.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Armed Silence - the Cocked Gate - near-silence at rest, requiring two agreeing harm channels to fire.

◦ Latched Alarm - the Held Alarm - a sustained on-state, kept alight by dynorphin after spiking stops.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Cocked Gate, predicted high input resistance of 250 to 500 megohms and near-silence, with dendritic coincidence detection requiring parabrachial pain and insular temperature or nausea signals together.

◦ In the Held Alarm the gate switches on and dynorphin sustains the state for a prolonged period; switching on takes more input than keeping it on, the defining hysteresis.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Two harm channels crossing threshold together - Cocked Gate to Held Alarm - milliseconds to trigger, then a slow peptide-held tail.

◦ Injury-driven potentiation of parabrachial input - lowers the requirement from two channels to one - days; the construct's central clinical claim.

◦ Decay of dynorphin tone - Held Alarm back to Cocked Gate - seconds to longer.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ The pathological direction is a gate latched on for weeks, in which a twinge, nausea or a bad memory each trigger the full defensive response alone; the intercalated cells enforcing the two-channel threshold are the predicted watchdog.


ENTRY 66. MEDIUM SPINY NEURONS, ALSO CALLED SPINY PROJECTION NEURONS (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 66)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is the archetypal two-state striatal switch: a deeply hyperpolarised cell that is deaf until convergent cortical input flips it into a plateau from which it can fire.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Down State - the Silent Floor - a hyperpolarised resting state near minus 85 millivolts held by inward-rectifier potassium channels, in which the cell ignores input.

◦ Up State - the Listening Plateau - a depolarised shelf near minus 55 to minus 50 millivolts lasting hundreds of milliseconds, the only state from which threshold is reached.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Silent Floor input resistance is low, ordinary inputs barely move the cell, and it fires under one spike per second.

◦ In the Listening Plateau, sustained by dendritic N-methyl-D-aspartate and L-type calcium plateaus, an A-type potassium current slows the first spike, and firing rises only to about 10 to 30 hertz during a movement the cell joins.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Strong synchronous cortical and thalamic input - Silent Floor to Listening Plateau - tens of milliseconds; requires agreement among thousands of weak inputs.

◦ Dopamine on spine necks - biases the flip and tags eligible synapses - tens to hundreds of milliseconds after the causing event, solving credit assignment.

◦ Loss of drive and inward-rectifier clamp - Listening Plateau back to Silent Floor - hundreds of milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ The standing energy cost of holding minus 85 millivolts against a leaking potassium channel makes this the first cell to die when metabolism fails, as in Huntington's disease and carbon monoxide poisoning; the watchdog is dense vascularisation and astrocytic lactate supply, and the boundary signature is calcium overload once the plateau cannot be paid for.


ENTRY 67. D1-TYPE MEDIUM SPINY NEURONS OF THE DIRECT PATHWAY (Go-Signal Movement Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 67)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a dopamine-gated version of the two-state striatal switch, biased toward a deeper down state and unlocked by dopamine acting through a slow chemical bistable switch bolted onto the membrane.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Deep Down State - the Locked Floor - a resting state made harder to leave by a stronger inward rectifier than the D2 cell.

◦ Dopamine-Enabled Up State - the Green Light - a plateau and burst that the same cortical input produces only once dopamine has raised excitability.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Locked Floor the cell needs more injected current to spike, has lower input resistance, and fires below one spike per second.

◦ In the Green Light, D1 receptor and protein kinase A signalling raises L-type calcium current through Cav1.2 and Cav1.3, boosts N-methyl-D-aspartate current and cuts potassium current, producing bursts of 10 to 40 hertz locked to movement onset.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Dopamine surge coincident with cortical drive - Locked Floor to Green Light - hundreds of milliseconds through the cyclic AMP and protein kinase A and DARPP-32 cascade; the reward broadcast is set from above.

◦ Dopamine arriving within about 0.3 to 2 seconds of coincident activity - converts an eligibility tag into long-term potentiation - seconds.

◦ Loss of dopamine and cortical drive - back to Locked Floor - hundreds of milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Without dopamine the cell stays locked and the animal is akinetic (unable to initiate movement); the DARPP-32 to protein phosphatase 1 bistable switch is the watchdog holding recent dopamine memory, and dynorphin negative feedback limits over-drive.


ENTRY 68. D2-TYPE MEDIUM SPINY NEURONS OF THE INDIRECT PATHWAY (Stop-Signal Movement Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 68)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is the more excitable sibling of the striatal switch, the point where dopamine and adenosine fight to set its up-state threshold.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Excitable Down State - the Ready Floor - a resting state with weaker inward rectifier, higher input resistance and lower rheobase than the D1 cell, so it leaves rest more easily.

◦ Up-State Firing - the Red Light - a plateau and burst that delivers the stop command to the external pallidum.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Ready Floor the cell rests near minus 80 to minus 85 millivolts but produces more spikes for the same current than the D1 cell.

◦ In the Red Light, up-state firing drives strongly depressing perisomatic pallidal baskets, so the first spikes of a burst dominate; dopamine D2 signalling damps the cell while adenosine A2A signalling excites it, setting a ratio rather than a level.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Convergent cortical pyramidal-tract and thalamic input - Ready Floor to Red Light - tens of milliseconds.

◦ Dopamine D2 versus adenosine A2A balance - sets how readily the up state fires - seconds; energy state and neuromodulation, partly set from above.

◦ Dopamine dip favouring long-term potentiation - biases the cell more excitable - seconds and longer.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Dopamine loss removes the D2 brake and produces chronic over-activity, calcium loading and 20 to 40 percent spine loss, the fault seen in Parkinson's disease, while preferential loss of these cells in early Huntington's disease produces chorea; enkephalin auto-gain-control on the terminals is the watchdog that stops the stop pathway running away.


ENTRY 69. STRIATAL CHOLINERGIC INTERNEURONS, ALSO CALLED TONICALLY ACTIVE NEURONS (Steady-Beat Surprise-Pause Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 69)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is an always-on autonomous pacemaker whose signal is a punctuation mark: a steady beat interrupted by a precisely timed pause that opens the striatum's learning window.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Tonic Pacemaking - the Background Beat - continuous autonomous firing that bathes the striatum in acetylcholine.

◦ Burst-Pause - the Surprise Pause - a brief excitation followed by a silence timed to salient events.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Background Beat the cell rests near minus 60 millivolts and self-fires at a few hertz, driven by the hyperpolarisation-activated cyclic nucleotide-gated (h) current and a persistent sodium current, each broad spike followed by a 100 to 300 millisecond afterhyperpolarisation that sets the slow rate.

◦ In the Surprise Pause a thalamic burst loads a Kv1-family potassium current and, together with a coincident dopamine surge on D2 receptors, produces a silence about 200 milliseconds wide that permits endocannabinoid-dependent long-term depression while acetylcholine falls from M2 and M4 receptors.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Salient or unexpected event reported by centromedian and parafascicular thalamus, plus dopamine - Background Beat to Surprise Pause - the pause follows within tens of milliseconds.

◦ Kv1 recovery and h-current re-engagement - Surprise Pause back to Background Beat - a couple of hundred milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Depolarisation is normally avoided; the clinical fault is cell loss, with a reported 50 to 60 percent reduction in severe Tourette syndrome, whose signature is a striatum that treats surprising and ordinary events alike; acetylcholinesterase at extreme striatal density is the watchdog that keeps the volume-transmitted signal from becoming a permanent bath.


ENTRY 70. STRIATAL PARVALBUMIN FAST-SPIKING INTERNEURONS (Split-Second Impulse-Curbing Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 70)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a fast-spiking feedforward veto with a network twist: gap junctions let the population switch between independent local vetoes and one coordinated region-wide veto.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Fast-Spiking Veto - the Split-Second Curb - sustained high-frequency firing that clamps spiny neuron onset within a few milliseconds of a cortical volley.

◦ Stuttering - the Flicker - irregular high-frequency runs separated by unpredictable pauses at drive just above threshold.

◦ Gap-Junction Synchrony - the Shared Veto - a coupled network state in which coupled cells lock together and deliver one region-wide inhibition.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Split-Second Curb the cell fires above 200 hertz with little slowing thanks to Kv3 potassium channels, spikes under half a millisecond wide, in the behaving animal 10 to 20 hertz between events and higher during movement.

◦ In the Flicker a slowly recovering Kv1 current chops firing into runs and pauses.

◦ In the Shared Veto, connexin-36 gap junctions to 30 to 40 percent of neighbours share subthreshold voltage and synchronise without any chemical synapse firing.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Strong synchronous cortical drive - independent Split-Second Curb to Shared Veto - milliseconds; how hard cortex drives the network decides which.

◦ Drive just above threshold - into the Flicker - milliseconds.

◦ Dopamine setting gap-junction strength - tunes synchrony - seconds; a gain service set from above.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Selectively killing these cells widens the coincidence window and produces dyskinesias, excess grooming and anxiety (a Tourette-like fault); their extreme oxidative load makes them vulnerable in schizophrenia, ischaemia and ageing, and the perineuronal net is the protective watchdog buffering ions and shielding against oxidative damage.


ENTRY 71. STRIATAL SOMATOSTATIN, NITRIC OXIDE SYNTHASE, AND NEUROPEPTIDE Y LOW-THRESHOLD-SPIKING INTERNEURONS (Free-Spreading Messenger Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 71)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a hair-trigger plateau generator that answers a brief weak input with a long self-sustaining reply, and its most important output is a diffusing gas rather than spikes.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Spontaneous Tonic - the Idle Trickle - self-firing at 1 to 10 hertz from a depolarised rest near minus 56 millivolts.

◦ Low-Threshold Calcium Burst - the Rebound Reply - a T-type calcium spike fired on release from hyperpolarisation.

◦ Plateau Firing - the Long Answer - a persistent-sodium plateau lasting hundreds of milliseconds during which a ragged spike train and nitric oxide release proceed.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Idle Trickle the very high input resistance (500 to 1,000 megohms and more) makes tiny inputs move the cell far.

◦ In the Rebound Reply, T-type channels must first be de-inactivated by prior hyperpolarisation before the slow calcium hump fires.

◦ In the Long Answer the plateau sustains firing and drives nitric oxide synthesis, a messenger with a 100 to 200 micrometre sphere of influence and no synapse.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Brief cortical or thalamic input after a hyperpolarising dip - Idle Trickle to Rebound Reply to Long Answer - milliseconds to enter, hundreds of milliseconds to hold.

◦ Cholinergic interneuron excitation, reciprocated - biases the cell toward plateau - milliseconds; serotonin from dorsal raphe strongly inhibits it.

◦ Dopamine on D1 and D5 receptors - triggers nitric oxide release for long-term depression - seconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Despite working N-methyl-D-aspartate receptors the cell resists excitotoxic death and is one of the few striatal populations spared in Huntington's disease, so its notable feature is the absence of a runaway fault; the watchdog is its own failure to generate damaging mitochondrial reactive oxygen species when overstimulated.


ENTRY 72. STRIATAL CALRETININ INTERNEURONS (Human-Enriched Quiet Sentinel Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 72)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is the least electrically characterised striatal interneuron, so the honest register is minimal: a low-threshold adapting cell delivering soft distributed inhibition, with a low-confidence flag throughout.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Low-Threshold Tonic - the Quiet Sentinel - a low-rate adapting discharge providing gentle background inhibition that keeps spiny-neuron ensembles from rising together.

◦ Sag-Rebound - the Slow Drift - a modest hyperpolarisation-activated cyclic nucleotide-gated (HCN) sag and rebound on release from inhibition.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Quiet Sentinel the cell shows high input resistance of 300 to 800 megohms, narrow spikes of 0.6 to 1.0 milliseconds, rests near minus 70 to minus 60 millivolts, adapts, and modulates rather than vetoes because it avoids the axon initial segment; confidence low.

◦ In the Slow Drift the HCN sag produces a gentle rebound after hyperpolarisation; the large Lhx7-positive primate subtype instead releases acetylcholine as a modulator.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Cortical, intralaminar thalamic or dopamine input - rest to Quiet Sentinel - milliseconds.

◦ Release from inhibition - into the Slow Drift rebound - tens to hundreds of milliseconds.

◦ Mutual loop with cholinergic interneurons - sets background acetylcholine tone - seconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ No characterised pathological firing mode exists, and inventing one would be dishonest; the one documented change is preservation of these cells while spiny neurons die in Huntington's disease, so their fractional share rises as the striatum shrinks.


ENTRY 73. STRIATAL TYROSINE HYDROXYLASE-EXPRESSING INTERNEURONS (Dopamine-Marked Inhibitory Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 73)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a bistable latching brake: a quiet cell with two stable membrane states, flipped by a brief input, that commits to a decision and holds it for up to a second.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Quiet Down State - the Waiting Latch - a near-silent state near minus 75 millivolts with exceptionally high input resistance (600 megohms to over 1 gigaohm).

◦ Plateau Potential - the Held Decision - a depolarised state near minus 40 millivolts lasting 200 to 1,500 milliseconds during which the cell fires slowly.

◦ Depolarisation Block - the Jammed On - a used continuation of the plateau in which the cell is too depolarised to reset sodium channels and stops spiking while staying depolarised.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Waiting Latch a few tens of picoamperes move the cell tens of millivolts, so it sits quiet until pushed past about minus 55 millivolts.

◦ In the Held Decision a persistent sodium current and a calcium-activated non-selective cation current sustain the plateau; D1 and D5 receptor activation raises cyclic AMP and lengthens it, a rare directly testable case of a transmitter setting a cell's mode.

◦ In the Jammed On, GABAergic output persists as the membrane stays depolarised at plateau's end.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Brief cortical or parafascicular thalamic input - Waiting Latch to Held Decision - milliseconds to flip, up to a second to hold.

◦ Dopamine D1 and D5 tone - lengthens the plateau - seconds; set from above.

◦ Sodium-channel inactivation at plateau end - Held Decision to Jammed On and then reset - up to a second.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Depolarisation block is used here, so it belongs in the register, not the fault list; the pathological direction is phenotypic, a failed compensation in which dopamine denervation multiplies these cells without restoring dopamine release, and proteasomal degradation of tyrosine hydroxylase is the housekeeping watchdog on enzyme level.


ENTRY 74. GLOBUS PALLIDUS PROTOTYPIC NEURONS (Nonstop Braking Relay Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 74)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a clock, not an integrator: a high-rate autonomous pacemaker whose informative signal is a pause in an otherwise nonstop tonic hum, and whose computation is phase advance and delay.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Tonic Pacemaking - the Nonstop Brake - continuous regular high-rate firing that keeps the subthalamic nucleus leashed.

◦ Striatal-Driven Pause - the Gap - a brief silence imposed by an indirect-pathway striatal volley, read against the tonic hum.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Nonstop Brake the cell rests depolarised near minus 55 millivolts and self-fires 40 to 70 hertz in the awake primate, driven by persistent sodium and hyperpolarisation-activated cyclic nucleotide-gated currents, spikes 0.4 to 0.7 milliseconds wide, low input resistance of 60 to 200 megohms, almost no adaptation.

◦ In the Gap, strong depressing striatopallidal inhibition briefly overrides the pacemaker; the information is the rate decrease, not the spike.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Indirect-pathway striatal burst - Nonstop Brake to Gap - the pause reaches the subthalamic nucleus within 1 to 3 milliseconds via fast myelinated axons.

◦ End of striatal drive and pacemaker recovery - Gap back to Nonstop Brake - tens of milliseconds.

◦ Dopamine loss - reshapes tonic firing into beta-locked bursts - days; a maladaptive shift thrown by the wider circuit state.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ The pathological regime is bursty beta-band synchrony after dopamine loss, whose boundary signature is loss of reliable autonomous firing and coupling with the subthalamic nucleus; local axon collateral mutual inhibition and low-level gap-junction coupling are the desynchronising watchdogs, and iron and manganese handling by pallidal glia is a metabolic vulnerability.


ENTRY 75. GLOBUS PALLIDUS ARKYPALLIDAL NEURONS (Backward-Reaching Cancel Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 75)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a slow, irregular broadcast canceller: everything about it is looser than its prototypic neighbour, and it computes by broadcast timing, implementing a race with a deadline rather than a sum.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Slow Irregular Baseline - the Idle Watch - low-rate irregular firing that keeps the striatal broadcast quiet.

◦ Cancel Burst - the Backward Shout - a high-frequency burst that floods the entire striatum with weak simultaneous inhibition to abort a developing action.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Idle Watch the cell rests near minus 65 to minus 58 millivolts and fires 3 to 15 hertz irregularly, spikes 0.8 to 1.2 milliseconds wide, input resistance 150 to 400 megohms.

◦ In the Backward Shout a strong striatal-driven burst spreads through sparse terminals and gamma-aminobutyric acid spillover onto extrasynaptic (delta-subunit) receptors, producing a tonic conductance that shifts the whole striatum's gain rather than vetoing one cell.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ A stop or cancel cue relayed through striatum and subthalamic drive - Idle Watch to Backward Shout - the effect takes tens of milliseconds because unmyelinated terminals dominate the delay.

◦ Slow cortical oscillation of sleep and anaesthesia - holds arkypallidal and prototypic cells in antiphase - cycle by cycle; a brain-state service from above.

◦ Dopamine depletion - raises firing and cortical entrainment - days.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ In parkinsonian animals the cancel key is pressed too often, contributing to difficulty initiating movement; astrocytic gamma-aminobutyric acid uptake in the striatum sets the strength and spread of the stop signal and is the watchdog that keeps the broadcast bounded.


ENTRY 76. SUBTHALAMIC NUCLEUS NEURONS (Sudden-Halt Excitatory Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 76)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a mode-rich autonomous pacemaker: it fires with no input at all, rebounds into bursts after inhibition, and is multistable, so its baseline must be modelled explicitly.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Autonomous Pacemaking - the Self-Running Clock - regular single-spike firing that continues even with every synapse blocked.

◦ Rebound Burst - the Recoil Volley - a rapid volley fired when pallidal inhibition lifts.

◦ Multistable Persistent Firing - the Held Gear - more than one persistent firing state for the same input, documented in human surgical recordings.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Self-Running Clock the cell fires 10 to 30 hertz in vivo, driven by a persistent sodium current and a slow calcium-activated potassium current.

◦ In the Recoil Volley, prior pallidal inhibition de-inactivates T-type calcium channels, and their release fires a burst far above baseline.

◦ In the Held Gear the same synaptic input can leave the cell in different persistent states depending on history, through dendritic calcium electrogenesis.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Hyperdirect cortical drive - transiently raises rate from the Self-Running Clock - cortex-to-subthalamic delay about 3 to 5 milliseconds.

◦ Pallidal inhibition then release - Self-Running Clock to Recoil Volley - the pallido-subthalamic loop delay of about 5 to 10 milliseconds each way sets the resonance.

◦ Dopamine loss - locks the loop into beta rhythm of 13 to 35 hertz - days; a state thrown by the wider circuit.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Rebound bursting is used normally, so it stays in the register; the pathological direction is pathological beta synchrony driving parkinsonian rigidity, and destroying the nucleus produces hemiballismus (violent flinging movements), so its excitation is the safety hold; astrocytic adenosine release during high-frequency stimulation is a natural damping watchdog.


ENTRY 77. SUBSTANTIA NIGRA PARS RETICULATA NEURONS (Standing-Brake Release Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 77)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is among the fastest sustained firers in the brain, an autonomous pacemaker whose informative signal is the pause, so any model must treat rate decreases as the carrier.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ High-Rate Tonic - the Standing Brake - nonstop fast firing that holds thalamus, colliculus and brainstem targets suppressed.

◦ Brake-Release Pause - the Let-Go - a brief rate drop, driven by direct-pathway striatal input, that releases a target movement.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Standing Brake the cell self-fires at a median near 68 hertz (30 to 100 hertz), spikes under 1 millisecond wide with brief afterhyperpolarisation and almost no adaptation, driven by a persistent sodium current.

◦ In the Let-Go, strongly depressing direct-pathway striatal input briefly silences the cell; the same synapse can produce suppression in one thalamic target and rebound excitation in another, so output sign is target-dependent.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Direct-pathway striatal burst - Standing Brake to Let-Go - the pause reaches the superior colliculus within a few milliseconds.

◦ Hydrogen peroxide on adenosine triphosphate (ATP)-sensitive potassium channels - trims firing rate to the tissue's metabolic state - seconds; a metabolic service.

◦ End of striatal volley and pacemaker recovery - Let-Go back to Standing Brake - tens of milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Silencing the nucleus produces uncontrollable eye and limb movements; the nucleus is also the brain's seizure gate, so its loss removes a natural anticonvulsant circuit, and metabolic sensing through hydrogen peroxide and ATP-sensitive potassium channels is the built-in watchdog coupling firing to energy status.


ENTRY 78. SUBSTANTIA NIGRA PARS COMPACTA NEURONS (Movement-Vigor Supplying Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 78)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:

This is a great mode-rich neuromodulator cell: a slow calcium-driven pacemaker overlaid with reward bursts and disappointment pauses, whose message is a diffusing dopamine field.

• THE MODE REGISTER.

◦ Tonic Pacemaking - the Background Vigor - slow regular firing at 1 to 5 hertz that sets ambient dopamine and movement gain.

◦ Reward Burst - the Better-Than-Expected Volley - a 2 to 6 spike burst at 15 to 30 hertz that releases far more dopamine per spike.

◦ Disappointment Pause - the Worse-Than-Expected Silence - a 100 to 400 millisecond silence signalling a worse outcome than predicted.

• PER-MODE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS.

◦ In the Background Vigor, pacemaking is driven by Cav1.3 L-type calcium channels opening at negative voltages, broad spikes over 2 milliseconds with long afterhyperpolarisation.

◦ In the Better-Than-Expected Volley, glutamate on N-methyl-D-aspartate receptors switches the cell into the burst, limited by D2 autoreceptor self-inhibition.

◦ In the Worse-Than-Expected Silence, inhibition, notably from the rostromedial tegmental nucleus, halts firing.

• THE TRANSITION TABLE.

◦ Reward-predicting or surprising excitation (subthalamic, pedunculopontine, superior colliculus) - Background Vigor to Reward Burst - milliseconds.

◦ Omitted expected reward via rostromedial tegmental inhibition - to Disappointment Pause - milliseconds; a teaching signal from above.

◦ D2 autoreceptor feedback and clearance - back to Background Vigor - hundreds of milliseconds.

• FAULT REGIMES AND WATCHDOGS.

◦ Its calcium-driven pacemaker plus a 4.5-metre unmyelinated axon and oxidising dopamine make it plausibly the most metabolically stressed neuron in the brain and the one that dies in Parkinson's disease; neuromelanin iron-chelation is the protective watchdog until overloaded, and alpha-synuclein misfolding makes its failure transmissible along its own connections.


ENTRY 79. VENTRAL TEGMENTAL AREA NEURONS (Reward-Pursuit Driving Neuron) (keyed to neuron catalogue Chapter 79)

MODES AND MODE TRANSITIONS:
